Like when somebody asks you a hard question and you start thinking about some old song on the radio instead. I’m sorry, I know that’s not really answering. I just… I can’t tell you straight right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. I wasn’t aiming for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel hungry much these days?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. I mean, I guess my stomach must be empty, but I don’t really feel like eating. It’s like I’ll just have coffee, maybe a piece of bread if it’s around, and then the day goes by. Then I’m messing with my pants ’cause they’re hanging loose, and I think, oh… I really didn’t eat again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I guess. I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest, like… like nothing good is waiting for me. I just want to keep my head down and not deal with things. It’s strange because mornings used to be when I’d get moving, you know, like my uncle used to say you gotta start before the sun gets too high—he had this old truck that never started right—but now I just wake up already feeling low.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of wanting to die or hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that straight. I get real tired of waking up feeling like this, you know? Like it’d be easier if I could just not have to keep doing the day. But hurt myself… I don’t know. My mind kind of wanders when you ask that. I was thinking about this guy I used to know who’d say, “Just sleep it off,” like sleep fixes everything, but it doesn’t. So… I don’t know, I guess I’ve had thoughts more like not wanting to be here, but I’m not sure what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Are you thinking about killing yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now? I… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve got it clear like that in my head. It’s more like everything feels too much, and then my mind kind of slides away from it. Like when somebody asks you a hard question and you start thinking about some old song on the radio instead. I don’t know if that means yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. When you say “want to,” it sounds so clear, like there’s a switch flipped, but mine doesn’t feel like that. It’s more like I’m standing there with all this noise in my head, and then I start thinking about dumb stuff, like the ceiling fan in my old place that clicked all night. I’m sorry, I know that’s not really answering. I just… I can’t tell you straight right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like somebody standing there talking to me, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes I get caught up in my own thoughts and it feels loud, like when a TV’s on in the other room and you can’t make out the words, but I don’t know if that counts. My old neighbor used to leave his radio on all night, and I’d swear I heard it even after he moved out, but that was probably just me remembering it. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed voices, no.\n\nDoctor: How much alcohol do you drink in a typical day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a typical day, I guess. Some days I don’t drink at all, or I don’t think about it, and then other times… I don’t know, it depends what’s around. Like, if there’s beer in the fridge, it’s different than if I have to go out, and I hate going into stores sometimes because the lights make everything feel like a hospital waiting room.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to count it. I couldn’t give you a clean number right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It’s strange because mornings used to be when I’d get moving, you know, like my uncle used to say you gotta start before the sun gets too high—he had this old truck that never started right—but now I just wake up already feeling low.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of wanting to die or hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that straight. So… I don’t know, I guess I’ve had thoughts more like not wanting to be here, but I’m not sure what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Are you thinking about killing yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now? I… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve got it clear like that in my head. It’s more like everything feels too much, and then my mind kind of slides away from it. Like when somebody asks you a hard question and you start thinking about some old song on the radio instead. I don’t know if that means yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. When you say “want to,” it sounds so clear, like there’s a switch flipped, but mine doesn’t feel like that. It’s more like I’m standing there with all this noise in my head, and then I start thinking about dumb stuff, like the ceiling fan in my old place that clicked all night. I’m sorry, I know that’s not really answering. I just… I can’t tell you straight right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like somebody standing there talking to me, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes I get caught up in my own thoughts and it feels loud, like when a TV’s on in the other room and you can’t make out the words, but I don’t know if that counts. My old neighbor used to leave his radio on all night, and I’d swear I heard it even after he moved out, but that was probably just me remembering it. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed voices, no.\n\nDoctor: How much alcohol do you drink in a typical day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a typical day, I guess. I know it’s not the weekend, I don’t think—unless it is, because the buses run different and that always throws me off. My mom used to mark calendars with those little stickers, like for appointments and bills, and I keep meaning to do that, but I never do.\n\nSo… today? I don’t know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me where we are right now?\n\nPatient: We’re… here, I mean, in this room with you. It’s strange because mornings used to be when I’d get moving, you know, like my uncle used to say you gotta start before the sun gets too high—he had this old truck that never started right—but now I just wake up already feeling low.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of wanting to die or hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that straight. I get real tired of waking up feeling like this, you know? Like it’d be easier if I could just not have to keep doing the day. But hurt myself… I don’t know. My mind kind of wanders when you ask that. I was thinking about this guy I used to know who’d say, “Just sleep it off,” like sleep fixes everything, but it doesn’t. So… I don’t know, I guess I’ve had thoughts more like not wanting to be here, but I’m not sure what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Are you thinking about killing yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now? I… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve got it clear like that in my head. It’s more like everything feels too much, and then my mind kind of slides away from it. Like when somebody asks you a hard question and you start thinking about some old song on the radio instead. I don’t know if that means yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. When you say “want to,” it sounds so clear, like there’s a switch flipped, but mine doesn’t feel like that. It’s more like I’m standing there with all this noise in my head, and then I start thinking about dumb stuff, like the ceiling fan in my old place that clicked all night. I’m sorry, I know that’s not really answering. I just… I can’t tell you straight right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like somebody standing there talking to me, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes I get caught up in my own thoughts and it feels loud, like when a TV’s on in the other room and you can’t make out the words, but I don’t know if that counts. My old neighbor used to leave his radio on all night, and I’d swear I heard it even after he moved out, but that was probably just me remembering it. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed voices, no.\n\nDoctor: How much alcohol do you drink in a typical day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a typical day, I guess. Some days I don’t drink at all, or I don’t think about it, and then other times… I don’t know, it depends what’s around. Like, if there’s beer in the fridge, it’s different than if I have to go out, and I hate going into stores sometimes because the lights make everything feel like a hospital waiting room.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to count it. I couldn’t give you a clean number right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had shaking when you stopped drinking?\n\nPatient: Shaking? I mean… I don’t know. My hands shake sometimes when I’m worked up, like when I’m trying to hold a cup and I start thinking about dropping it, and then of course I notice it more. But if you mean from stopping drinking specifically, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.\n\nIt reminds me of my uncle, he used to tap his cigarette ash everywhere and his fingers were always moving, drove my aunt crazy… Sorry. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: What day is it today?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I was trying to keep track, but the days kind of run together. I know it’s not the weekend, I don’t think—unless it is, because the buses run different and that always throws me off. My mom used to mark calendars with those little stickers, like for appointments and bills, and I keep meaning to do that, but I never do.\n\nSo… today? I don’t know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me where we are right now?\n\nPatient: We’re… here, I mean, in this room with you. I know it’s a doctor place, or appointment place, but I don’t know the name of it right now. The walls all look kind of the same in these buildings, and the chairs always make that squeak—my school had chairs like that, actually, the plastic ones that stuck to your legs in summer.\n\nSorry… where exactly? I’m not sure what this place is called.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what you ate yesterday?\n\nPatient: Yesterday… I’m trying to think. I had coffee, I know that, because the mug was still by the sink. Maybe a piece of bread? That’s been kind of most days lately, honestly. I just don’t eat right anymore.\n\nMy pants are getting loose, too—like they hang on me now. I used to have a belt with this old buckle, my dad gave it to me, and I keep meaning to find it… but yeah, yesterday was probably just coffee and bread, if that.\n\nDoctor: What is the last thing you remember eating today?\n\nPatient: Today… I think it was coffee. Maybe I had bread too, just a piece, but I can’t remember if that was today or yesterday.\n\nI keep doing that—like I’ll make coffee and then forget to eat anything real. My pants are loose now, they kind of hang off me, and I notice it when I’m walking because I have to keep pulling them up. I should eat better, I know, but… yeah, last thing today was probably just coffee, maybe bread.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it was after I got fired. That’s when it started getting weird, like first I felt all driven, like I had to do something, had to prove something, and then it just swung the other way and I’ve been really low.\n\nIt’s been going on about a month, I guess. Time’s been kind of slippery, like the days stack up and I can’t tell which was which… but about a month since that happened.\n\nDoctor: After you got fired, was there a time when you felt unusually energized or keyed up?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, there was. For a while after that I couldn’t sit still. I kept messing with my tools, cleaning them over and over, moving boxes from one side of the room to the other like it mattered.\n\nAnd I was making all these plans for jobs I was gonna do—like fixing things, picking up work, whatever came into my head. It felt like I had a motor running in me, like if I stopped moving something bad would happen or I’d lose the thread. My dad used to say a man’s tools tell you if he’s got his life together, so I kept lining them up… but yeah, I was really keyed up.\n\nDoctor: During that keyed-up time, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I wasn’t sleeping much at all. Maybe two, three hours, and then I’d pop back up like I’d slept all night.\n\nI remember looking at the clock and thinking it was too early to be awake, but then I’d start doing things anyway. My old clock has this little ticking sound—used to drive me nuts when I was a kid too—but back then I didn’t feel tired like I should’ve. Just up again, moving around.\n\nDoctor: During that keyed-up time, did you feel unusually confident or like you could do things other people couldn’t?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did. I was telling people I could start my own handyman thing easy, like no problem, like I’d be better off without that place anyway.\n\nI kept saying I was gonna show that building they made a big mistake letting me go. Like they’d see my name on a truck or something and feel stupid. I don’t know… I was talking big. I don’t usually talk like that, not really.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s like I’ll just have coffee, maybe a piece of bread if it’s around, and then the day goes by. Then I’m messing with my pants ’cause they’re hanging loose, and I think, oh… I really didn’t eat again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I guess. I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest, like… like nothing good is waiting for me. I just want to keep my head down and not deal with things. It’s strange because mornings used to be when I’d get moving, you know, like my uncle used to say you gotta start before the sun gets too high—he had this old truck that never started right—but now I just wake up already feeling low.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of wanting to die or hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that straight. I get real tired of waking up feeling like this, you know? Like it’d be easier if I could just not have to keep doing the day. But hurt myself… I don’t know. My mind kind of wanders when you ask that. I was thinking about this guy I used to know who’d say, “Just sleep it off,” like sleep fixes everything, but it doesn’t. So… I don’t know, I guess I’ve had thoughts more like not wanting to be here, but I’m not sure what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Are you thinking about killing yourself right now?\n\nPatient: Right now? I… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve got it clear like that in my head. It’s more like everything feels too much, and then my mind kind of slides away from it. Like when somebody asks you a hard question and you start thinking about some old song on the radio instead. I don’t know if that means yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to kill yourself right now?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. When you say “want to,” it sounds so clear, like there’s a switch flipped, but mine doesn’t feel like that. It’s more like I’m standing there with all this noise in my head, and then I start thinking about dumb stuff, like the ceiling fan in my old place that clicked all night. I’m sorry, I know that’s not really answering. I just… I can’t tell you straight right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like somebody standing there talking to me, if that’s what you mean. Sometimes I get caught up in my own thoughts and it feels loud, like when a TV’s on in the other room and you can’t make out the words, but I don’t know if that counts. My old neighbor used to leave his radio on all night, and I’d swear I heard it even after he moved out, but that was probably just me remembering it. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed voices, no.\n\nDoctor: How much alcohol do you drink in a typical day?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a typical day, I guess. Some days I don’t drink at all, or I don’t think about it, and then other times… I don’t know, it depends what’s around. Like, if there’s beer in the fridge, it’s different than if I have to go out, and I hate going into stores sometimes because the lights make everything feel like a hospital waiting room.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to count it. I couldn’t give you a clean number right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had shaking when you stopped drinking?\n\nPatient: Shaking? I mean… I don’t know. My hands shake sometimes when I’m worked up, like when I’m trying to hold a cup and I start thinking about dropping it, and then of course I notice it more. But if you mean from stopping drinking specifically, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.\n\nIt reminds me of my uncle, he used to tap his cigarette ash everywhere and his fingers were always moving, drove my aunt crazy… Sorry. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: What day is it today?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I was trying to keep track, but the days kind of run together. I know it’s not the weekend, I don’t think—unless it is, because the buses run different and that always throws me off. My mom used to mark calendars with those little stickers, like for appointments and bills, and I keep meaning to do that, but I never do.\n\nSo… today? I don’t know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me where we are right now?\n\nPatient: We’re… here, I mean, in this room with you. I know it’s a doctor place, or appointment place, but I don’t know the name of it right now. The walls all look kind of the same in these buildings, and the chairs always make that squeak—my school had chairs like that, actually, the plastic ones that stuck to your legs in summer.\n\nSorry… where exactly? I’m not sure what this place is called.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what you ate yesterday?\n\nPatient: Yesterday… I’m trying to think. I had coffee, I know that, because the mug was still by the sink. Maybe a piece of bread? That’s been kind of most days lately, honestly. I just don’t eat right anymore.\n\nMy pants are getting loose, too—like they hang on me now. I used to have a belt with this old buckle, my dad gave it to me, and I keep meaning to find it… but yeah, yesterday was probably just coffee and bread, if that.\n\nDoctor: What is the last thing you remember eating today?\n\nPatient: Today… I think it was coffee. Maybe I had bread too, just a piece, but I can’t remember if that was today or yesterday.\n\nI keep doing that—like I’ll make coffee and then forget to eat anything real. My pants are loose now, they kind of hang off me, and I notice it when I’m walking because I have to keep pulling them up. I should eat better, I know, but… yeah, last thing today was probably just coffee, maybe bread.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it was after I got fired. That’s when it started getting weird, like first I felt all driven, like I had to do something, had to prove something, and then it just swung the other way and I’ve been really low.\n\nIt’s been going on about a month, I guess. Time’s been kind of slippery, like the days stack up and I can’t tell which was which… but about a month since that happened.\n\nDoctor: After you got fired, was there a time when you felt unusually energized or keyed up?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, there was. For a while after that I couldn’t sit still. I kept messing with my tools, cleaning them over and over, moving boxes from one side of the room to the other like it mattered.\n\nAnd I was making all these plans for jobs I was gonna do—like fixing things, picking up work, whatever came into my head. It felt like I had a motor running in me, like if I stopped moving something bad would happen or I’d lose the thread. My dad used to say a man’s tools tell you if he’s got his life together, so I kept lining them up… but yeah, I was really keyed up.\n\nDoctor: During that keyed-up time, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I wasn’t sleeping much at all. Maybe two, three hours, and then I’d pop back up like I’d slept all night.\n\nI remember looking at the clock and thinking it was too early to be awake, but then I’d start doing things anyway. My old clock has this little ticking sound—used to drive me nuts when I was a kid too—but back then I didn’t feel tired like I should’ve. Just up again, moving around.\n\nDoctor: During that keyed-up time, did you feel unusually confident or like you could do things other people couldn’t?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I did. I was telling people I could start my own handyman thing easy, like no problem, like I’d be better off without that place anyway.\n\nI kept saying I was gonna show that building they made a big mistake letting me go. Like they’d see my name on a truck or something and feel stupid. I don’t know… I was talking big. I don’t usually talk like that, not really.\n\nDoctor: During that keyed-up time, did you have any beliefs that other people said were not true or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people did say I was being unrealistic. I kept telling them I could build my own handyman business easy, like I’d have a truck and jobs lined up and all that, and that the building was gonna regret firing me.\n\nI mean, my uncle used to do odd jobs out of the back of his car, had this old coffee can full of screws—anyway, I guess I was talking like it was all just gonna happen if I said it enough. People were like, “You don’t even have the work yet,” but I didn’t wanna hear that.\n\nDoctor: During that keyed-up time, were you talking a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I was. My sister told me I was talking so fast she could barely get a word in.\n\nI was going on about prices, making flyers, which tenants might need work done—like, “I can charge this, I can put these up here,” and then I’d jump to something else. She kept trying to say something, and I’d just keep rolling. I didn’t really notice it then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
